This is the *third* recorded instance of one class (impediment #184 for `2026-04-16-systemic-wiring-guide.md`, the THR-674 entry above for `.intent-proposals/`, this one for the protocol doc), and it is specifically the failure mode THR-686 item 2 was filed to close. It did not close it: the fix scoped the lint to dated `Docs/plans/20??-??-??-*.md`, and every one of these three offenders either *is* date-prefixed or is nested under `Docs/plans/`. **Method note worth keeping:** a non-staged `npm run lint:plan-doc` run showed nothing for this file — only the hook's `--staged` invocation surfaced it, so a pre-commit "clean lint" check that does not mirror the hook's exact arguments proves nothing. I had written "zero findings" into the commit body on the strength of the non-staged run and had to amend it. | ~5 min: scanning 31 findings to confirm none was genuine, then amending a commit message that had recorded a false verification claim. | S | Yes — advisory, non-blocking, so the commit landed; the cost is noise burying real findings and one falsified evidence line. | **Fix:** stop scoping by filename shape — it has now failed three times against three different document types. Use the frontmatter opt-out THR-686 already built (`> **lint_plan_doc:** exempt`) and apply it to the standing docs (`2026-04-13-linear-coordination-protocol.md`, `2026-04-16-systemic-wiring-guide.md`), plus a directory-level skip for `.intent-proposals/`. The distinguishing property is "is this a design plan doc?", which is a *declared* property, not one recoverable from the filename. Tracked adjacent to THR-692. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-688.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-689, hourly pickup) — testing (S): an engine test that "runs N ticks" has two invisible ceilings — the doom clock and vitest's 5s default — and the first one silently makes a correct guard-rail assertion false.** Writing the cap regression for `__DEBUG.tick(n)`, the natural assertion for `DEBUG_TICK_MAX = 200` is `expect(result.ticksRun).toBe(200)`. It failed at **185**: on seed 42 / `MAP_SIZE_PRESETS.small` the doom clock ends the run and the state leaves `phase: 'playing'` around tick 185, so the batch correctly stopped early and reported `stoppedReason: 'phase_left_playing'`. The implementation was right and the *test* was wrong — it had coupled a guard-rail assertion to doom-clock pacing. The failure reads like a cap-arithmetic bug, which is the trap: the obvious next move is to "fix" the clamp. Second ceiling, hit immediately after: ~185 real ticks takes **~8s**, over vitest's 5s per-test default, so the corrected test then timed out and needed an explicit `}, 60_000)`. | ~10 min across two red runs before the doom-clock ceiling was recognised as the cause rather than the clamp. | S | Yes | **Workaround:** for any test bounded by a tick count, assert the *guarantee* (`toBeLessThanOrEqual(CAP)`) rather than an exact count, and add an independent witness that the loop is bounded — here, a counting `resolveTargets` callback invoked once per attempted tick, which proves boundedness without depending on how far the sim actually got. Give any test running >~50 real ticks an explicit timeout. **Method note:** when a tick-count assertion fails just under its target, check `state.phase` before suspecting the bound — a run that legitimately ended is indistinguishable from a miscounted loop if you only look at the number. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-689 cap regression test.

**New 2026-07-21 (THR-691, hourly pickup) — tooling (M): GitHub's server-side merge ignores `.gitattributes` merge drivers entirely — including the *built-in* `union` — so a `merge=union` attribute cannot keep a PR out of `CONFLICTING`.** THR-691's scope reasoned that GitHub declines *custom* merge drivers (which require a local `git config merge.<name>.driver` GitHub cannot run) but might honour `union`, since it is built into git itself and needs no local configuration. Measured directly rather than assumed, per the handoff's explicit instruction: with `Docs/changelog.md merge=union` committed and live **on the base branch**, two branches appending different rows at the same line produced `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/merges` → **`409 Merge conflict`**, and an equivalent PR read **`mergeable: CONFLICTING`, `mergeStateStatus: DIRTY`** — the precise THR-668 symptom the attribute was meant to prevent. The same branch pair merges **clean (exit 0, both rows, header intact)** locally. Conclusion: GitHub's merge machinery does not consult `.gitattributes` at all; the built-in-vs-custom distinction is irrelevant to it. | The attribute delivers no GitHub-side benefit, so the web "Resolve conflicts" button and auto-merge remain unable to settle closeout-doc conflicts. Had this not been measured, the ticket would have shipped as a silent partial fix and the next idled PR would have gone CONFLICTING anyway, with a plausible-looking `.gitattributes` entry suggesting it should not have. | M | Yes | **Workaround (now documented, not rediscovered per-run):** resolve locally and push — `git fetch origin main && git merge origin/main && git push`. Union then auto-resolves the three closeout docs and the PR leaves CONFLICTING. Written up in `.claude/skills/pull-work/SKILL.md` § "Closeout — resolving a conflicted closeout-docs PR" with the duplicate-row caveat (union keeps *both* sides of every conflicting hunk — right for an appended row, wrong for an edited one, so skim the diff) and the `Docs/project-status.md` exclusion. **Method note worth reusing:** a PR whose base is a scratch branch rather than `main` triggers no CI here (`ci.yml` fires only on `main`), so GitHub-side merge behaviour can be probed empirically for zero Actions minutes. **Second method note:** the first local demo was invalid — a single union merge exiting 0 proves nothing, because git's `ort` strategy frequently merges adjacent insertions cleanly on its own. Any claim that a merge attribute is load-bearing needs the A/B control with the attribute absent. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-691 union-merge verification.

**New 2026-07-21 (encounter-scene-integration design session) — stale-reference (S): the design-audit-pipeline's own inputs have drifted — all three auditors hit fallbacks that worked but cost signal.** Three independent hits in one pipeline run: (1) `Docs/design-brief.md` has no `§ NFPs` section (it carries a different "Six principles" rubric) and its `last_validated_against: 2026-06-11` is 40 days past the 14-day staleness threshold — the NFP auditor fell back to CLAUDE.md per its prompt and tagged `[design-brief-stale]`. (2) `.claude/skills/design-audit-pipeline/agents/vision-prompt.md` points auditors at `Design/Vision/*` — the actual Vision files live at `TheFantasyWorldSimulator/Vision/` (the auditor rediscovered the correct path via design-brief line 64; every future vision audit repeats this detour until the template is fixed). (3) `taste-profile.md` exists at neither path nor anywhere in the repo, so the taste-profile qualitative check is structurally unassessable — the prompt names a file that does not exist. All three fallbacks degraded gracefully (audits completed, verdicts rendered), which is exactly why nobody has noticed: the pipeline reports PASS while quietly auditing against less than it advertises. | ~0 min this session (auditors self-recovered); recurring detour cost on every future design-audit run. | S | Yes — every design-audit-pipeline invocation until fixed. | **Suggested fix (one small ticket):** correct the path in `vision-prompt.md`, either restore/relocate `taste-profile.md` or delete the check from the prompt, and either refresh `design-brief.md`'s NFP section or repoint the NFP prompt at CLAUDE.md permanently. | Encounter-scene-integration design session (Fable), 2026-07-21.

**New 2026-07-22 (THR-692, hourly pickup) — tooling (M): armed auto-merge does not self-heal a `BEHIND` branch under strict branch protection, so an "arm and forget" PR can idle indefinitely with every check green.** Resuming THR-692 found PR #696 open ~10h after the prior run reported "auto-merge armed, CI green" — an accurate report. All required checks *were* `SUCCESS` (`Test · Typecheck · Build`, Vercel), auto-merge *was* enabled, and `mergeable` was `MERGEABLE` with **no conflict**. The blocker was `mergeStateStatus: BEHIND`: `main` had advanced two commits after the PR was armed, and branch protection runs in **strict mode** ("branches must be up to date before merging"), which this repo has had since THR-282. GitHub's auto-merge does *not* update a stale branch on its own unless the repo's "always suggest updating pull request branches" auto-update setting is on — it is not — so the PR sat waiting for a branch update no one was going to perform. This is a **distinct failure mode from impediment #135 and THR-691**, which both cover auto-merge stalling at `DIRTY`/`CONFLICTING` from append-only closeout-doc conflicts and are resolved by a local merge; here there is nothing to resolve, the branch is merely stale. A survey of the board found the class is not isolated: **9 of 15 open PRs carry auto-merge armed and unmerged**, the oldest from 2026-07-03 (#488), i.e. ~19 days of silent idling. | This run: ~5 min to diagnose, ~1 command to clear. Across the lane: unbounded — a PR in this state never merges and never reports failure, so its Linear issue never auto-closes and the next hourly run re-resumes an issue whose work is genuinely complete. | M | Yes | **Workaround:** `gh pr update-branch <N>` — a GitHub-side merge of the base into the head branch, no local tree needed and no worktree checkout required. It re-triggers CI; auto-merge then fires unattended once the required check is green. **Method note worth keeping:** `gh pr view --json mergeable,mergeStateStatus` returns **`UNKNOWN`/`UNKNOWN` on first call** — GitHub computes mergeability lazily and the first query only schedules the computation. A single call is therefore useless for this diagnosis; re-query until it resolves (2–3 calls, a few seconds apart) before concluding anything about a PR's mergeability. Reading the first `UNKNOWN` as "no information available" is what lets a `BEHIND` PR stay invisible. **Systemic fix worth a ticket:** the THR-675 "arm and move on" guidance is sound but incomplete — it needs either the repo's branch auto-update setting enabled (which makes "arm and forget" actually true), or a sweep that reconciles armed-but-unmerged PRs. Filed as THR-702, which also carries the 9-PR backlog for separate triage — most are `docs(plan): batch flush` artifacts from the `flush-plan-docs` task retired in THR-654 and may be obsolete rather than wanted, so they need a per-PR call rather than a bulk merge. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-692 resume.

**Recurrence 2026-07-22 (THR-696, hourly pickup) — tooling (S): the `BEHIND`-at-arm-time case fires on a *freshly opened* PR, not only on one that idled.** THR-702 documents armed auto-merge stalling at `mergeStateStatus: BEHIND` after a PR sits and `main` advances. This run hit it on a PR that was **seconds old**: the branch was verified at `origin/main` tip immediately before the work started (`git rev-list HEAD..origin/main --count` → 0), but `main` moved during the session, so `gh pr merge --auto --merge` armed straight into `BEHIND`. Cheap containment, already applied here: after arming, read `gh pr view <n> --json autoMergeRequest,mergeStateStatus,mergeable` **once** and run `gh pr update-branch` if the state is `BEHIND` — one extra call, no CI polling, and it moves the PR to `BLOCKED`/`MERGEABLE` (check pending) which auto-merge *can* resolve unattended. This strengthens THR-702's case: the fix belongs in the closeout path unconditionally, not as an idle-PR remedy, because branch-tip freshness at session start says nothing about freshness at arm time.

**Recurrence 2026-07-22 (THR-696, hourly pickup) — sandbox (S): the DoD's 1920×1080 screenshot remains structurally unobtainable for a game-view surface; two tools each supply half.** The Browser-pane MCP holds a true 1920×1080 viewport and drove the whole verification (DOM assertions, `__DEBUG` calls, shipped-module imports), but `computer{action:"screenshot"}` fails with `Screenshot timed out after 5s: the Browser pane is not displayed, so the page is not compositing frames` — a headless CC session has no displayed pane to composite. Claude-in-Chrome captures successfully but its window is capped by the side panel (1232×969 here, from a 1658×1304 viewport despite `resize_window(1920,1080)`). Workaround used: take the *assertions* at 1920×1080 in the Browser pane and the *image* in Claude-in-Chrome, stating the deviation explicitly in the PR body rather than implying the capture matched the contract. Worth a decision at retro: either the DoD names this split as the sanctioned procedure, or the screenshot clause drops the pixel requirement for game-view surfaces, since no currently available tool satisfies it and each run pays the rediscovery cost. **DECIDED 2026-07-26 (THR-754):** the first option — the DoD now names the split as sanctioned procedure rather than dropping the pixel requirement. The image may come from whichever tool can capture, its real dimensions are stated honestly, and the 1920×1080 contract is discharged by DOM assertion from the Browser pane; the deviation is declared as `Browser-verify substitution: <route> — <reason>` instead of being rediscovered per run.

**New 2026-07-22 (marathon session, THR-698) — tooling (M): `git worktree remove --force` follows a `node_modules` junction and empties the junction TARGET — it wiped the home tree's real `node_modules` mid-session.** A throwaway timing worktree got a `node_modules` junction to the home install (the documented pattern), and `git worktree remove --force` was run with the junction still in place. The recursive delete traversed the reparse point: the home tree's `node_modules` was left with 0 entries, breaking esbuild/vitest/vite for the home tree AND every other worktree junctioned to it (first symptom: the pre-commit hook failing with `'esbuild' is not recognized`, which masqueraded as the known PATH impediment). The junction-cleanup order rule was already written down in session memory and the checked worktree docs — the failure was skipping it for a "trivial" throwaway worktree. | ~12 min: misdiagnosis as the PATH issue, then a full `npm install` in the home tree to restore. Tracked files unaffected (`node_modules` is gitignored). | M | Yes — any session that junctions node_modules. | **Rule:** ALWAYS `cmd /c rmdir "<wt>
ode_modules"` (removes the reparse point only) BEFORE `git worktree remove --force`, for every worktree including throwaways; verify `node_modules\.bin\esbuild` still exists in the home tree after any worktree removal. | Marathon session 2026-07-22, THR-698 timing A/B.

**Recurrence 2026-07-22 (marathon session, THR-698) — tooling (S): `gh pr view` can report a PR as OPEN/BLOCKED for ~40 minutes after it actually MERGED — the lazy-compute caveat extends to the whole merge-state read, not just the first UNKNOWN.** PR #707 was read as `state: OPEN, mergeStateStatus: BLOCKED, Test·Typecheck·Build IN_PROGRESS` across four separate `gh pr view` calls spanning half an hour — while the Actions API showed the same run's job `completed`/`success` and the PR had in fact merged at 09:22Z. The THR-702 method note ("re-query 2–3 times before concluding") covers the first-UNKNOWN case; this shows stale positives persist far longer. **Workaround:** when a PR seems stuck but its checks look done or timing is anomalous, cross-check the Actions run (`gh api .../actions/runs/<id>` → `status`/`conclusion`) or re-read `state` specifically — `state: MERGED` appeared only on the fifth read. | 210 | 1 | 2026-07-25 | process-friction | **The `keep-work-flowing-cc` Discord ping change-gate compares a SHA-256 of the "Needs Christian" items against a stored hash, but the skill never specifies the normalization — so each run invents its own pipeline and the hashes disagree even when the ask is byte-identical.** This run's stored hash was `e9d68463…`; recomputing over an unchanged, verbatim-carried ask gave `c8d7dc35…` (and `38a21b73…` under a trailing-newline variant of the same pipeline — `printf '%s' \| sha256sum` vs `… \| sha256sum` alone differ). The gate therefore reported CHANGED for an item whose text had not moved a character since the 10:10 brief. Proven rather than assumed: normalizing *both* briefings (10:10 and 11:09) through one identical pipeline produced the same digest, isolating the drift to the method, not the content. | The gate exists precisely to stop unchanged standing asks re-pinging hourly. Left alone it inverts into its own failure mode: a Discord DM to Christian **every hour** for an ask he has already seen — the exact notification fatigue THR-608's chat-only interface is meant to avoid — and the false pings are indistinguishable from real ones. | S | Yes | **Workaround this run:** do not trust the stored hash alone. Recompute the digest over the *previous* briefing's Needs-Christian section with the *same* pipeline used for the current one; if those two agree, the ask is genuinely unchanged and the ping is suppressed regardless of what the state file says. Then write the freshly-computed hash so the next run has a same-method baseline. **Durable fix:** pin the normalization in the skill (or a tiny committed script) so it is reproducible across runs — e.g. `awk` the section, keep only lines matching `^\s*[-*]\|^\*\*`, collapse whitespace, trim, then `sha256sum` with the trailing newline retained — and store the pipeline's identity alongside the digest so a future change of method is visibly a method change rather than a content change. | Hourly `keep-work-flowing-cc` run, 2026-07-25 11:09 brief. |
| 211 | 3 | 2026-07-26 | tooling | **Writing file content through `node -e` / `python - <<PY` inside a double-quoted shell string silently corrupts it two ways: backticks trigger command substitution, and a lone `\b` becomes a literal BACKSPACE (0x08) instead of the two characters `\` + `b`.** Both hit in one session. (a) Inserting a markdown paragraph containing inline code spans via `node -e "…"` ran every backtick span as a subshell — `Docs/project-status.md` was written with the code spans replaced by empty strings and a pile of `command not found` noise; the file had to be restored with `git checkout --`. (b) Patching a regex into a scratch harness produced `/<BACKSPACE>(?:you\|your)<BACKSPACE>/giu`, a regex that silently matches nothing. It cost four rounds to find, because every diagnostic lies: `grep` prints the backspace and the terminal *erases the preceding character*, so the line renders as the correct `\b` form; and `JSON.stringify` escapes a real backspace as `\b`, which is indistinguishable from a printed backslash-b. The detector it broke reported `0` hits — a plausible-looking answer, not an error. | Silent, and plausible in both directions. (a) mutated a tracked doc with no failure signal beyond stderr noise that is easy to skim past — the write "succeeded". (b) is worse: a broken detector in an audit harness returns a *believable* zero, so the finding it exists to surface (40 templates written in the wrong voice) would simply have been absent from the deliverable with nothing anywhere indicating a bug. Any audit, lint, or codemod built this way can under-report and read as a clean result. | S | Yes | **Workaround that worked:** never pass literary or regex content through a shell string. Write the content to a file with the `Write` tool (no shell layer at all), then have a tiny script read that file and splice it — `fs.readFileSync(scratch + "/entry.md", "utf8")`. For a single backslash inside a script that must be generated, build it as `String.fromCharCode(92) + "b"` rather than escaping through the shell→interpreter→string-literal stack. **Verify, do not eyeball:** confirm a written backslash with `line.charCodeAt(idx) === 92`; `grep`, `cat` and `JSON.stringify` all render 0x08 as if it were correct. Prefer `node --input-type=module -e '…'` with *single* quotes and `process.argv` for paths, which removes interpolation entirely. **Also noted:** `python3` is not on PATH in this sandbox (only `python`), and a `python3 - <<PY` heredoc fails with the Microsoft Store alias message rather than a missing-interpreter error — a `&&` chain after it still runs, so a patch step can appear to succeed while having done nothing. | THR-776 (Nudge Model WS3 migration audit) — audit harness authoring + closeout doc edits. |

(2) `__DEBUG` state assertion — read the live `GameState` via the React fiber walk and confirm `playerActionReceipts[0].presentation === 'modal'` with the authored reaction ids, then apply one and assert the resulting `hiddenMarks` entry and `reputationTallies` delta plus the `encounter_aftermath_effect` traces. Note the fiber walk was also needed just to *get an agent id*: `__DEBUG.fireAction` matches on `node.properties.name`, which is absent on seeded agents (the name lives on `node.name`), so every name lookup returns "No agent matching 'X'" and only the node id works. **Durable fix:** THR-754 is the open ticket to codify sanctioned headless evidence paths; this entry is one more instance for it. **New 2026-07-23 (THR-717, hourly pickup) — tooling (S): `check:process` reports a permanent false `deferred-ref` ERROR on drift-scan's own report template.** `scripts/drift-scan/index.ts` builds a markdown table that *documents* the patterns it counts, including a row rendering the literal text `// DEFERRED`. CI's `Process lint (advisory)` step is non-gating and passes, so nothing is blocked — but the gate reports a red error on every run, which is how a lint earns the habit of being ignored (the THR-686 decay). | ~4 min: `git show origin/main:<file> | grep` to prove pre-existence rather than assume it. | S | Yes — every `check:process` run, indefinitely. | **Workaround:** none needed; verified pre-existing and non-gating, so it was noted rather than chased. **Suggested fix:** exclude string/template literals from the `deferred-ref` scan, or skip `scripts/drift-scan/**` (the file that counts markers will always contain marker text). **Method note worth keeping:** when a repo-wide lint fires on a file you touched, diff the offending *line* against `origin/main` before investigating — it separates "I caused this" from "I moved this" in one command, and this one had only shifted by two lines. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-717 verification.

**New 2026-07-23 (THR-717, hourly pickup) — tooling (S): Node's `--experimental-strip-types` and `esbuild --bundle` disagree on import extensions, so a module shared between the two runners must use `.ts` explicitly.** The new `interface-contracts.ts` is imported by both `generate-interface-map.ts` (esbuild-bundled) and `scripts/drift-scan/interface-coverage.ts` (run by `node --experimental-strip-types`). Written with the extensionless `from './subsystems-registry'` that the surrounding esbuild-only scripts use, it built fine but the drift-scan path died with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` — Node's native ESM loader requires the extension, esbuild does not. The repo already contains both conventions (`generate-systems-inventory.ts` extensionless, `drift-scan/index.ts` with `.ts`), chosen implicitly by which runner each script happens to use, so the right convention for a *shared* module is not discoverable by looking at neighbours. Note this is the same root cause as the still-open THR-683 (`drift-scan` crashing on an extensionless `lint-rulebook` import). | ~5 min. | S | Yes — recurs for any future module shared across the two runners, and the repo is steadily adding `node --experimental-strip-types` scripts. | **Fix applied:** use `.ts` extensions on every import of the shared modules; `allowImportingTsExtensions: true` is already set in `tsconfig.node.json`, and esbuild resolves them fine, so `.ts` is the strictly safer default. **Suggested rule:** default new `scripts/` imports to explicit `.ts` — it works under both runners, whereas extensionless works under only one. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-717 implementation.

**New 2026-07-23 (THR-724, hourly pickup) — diagnosis-quality (M): a plan doc's dormancy diagnosis named the symptom ("content-starved") where the cause was a dead read site, and implementing the plan as written would have shipped nothing.** The THR-724 plan stated break 1 as "generation is content-starved: the only triggers are `completedEncounter.secretDiscovery` and the `secret_discovery` aftermath effect, and zero templates in the live pool author either" — remediation: add the metadata to ~10 live templates. That remediation alone would have produced exactly zero secrets, because the `completedEncounter.secretDiscovery` trigger lives in `phaseEncounterProgressionV2`, which iterates `state.encounterProgress` — a list that is **empty for the entire run** (the live pipeline resolves `state.unifiedActions`). The grep that found the trigger could not see that the collection it walks is never populated. Two further inert paths sat underneath: `encounter-content.ts`'s `toUnifiedTemplate` dropped both `secretDiscovery` and `favorGeneration`, and `favorGeneration` was not on `UnifiedActionTemplate` at all, so the two authored `favorGeneration` entries in the file had never done anything either. | ~35 min: instrumenting the CLI to find that no completed action in 120 ticks targets an actor, then tracing back to `encounterProgress.length === 0`. Cheap only because the plan supplied a runtime baseline (0 edges/120 ticks) to disprove against. | M | Yes — this is the general shape of a "dormant system" diagnosis. A symbol grep proves a call site *exists*; it says nothing about whether the call site *executes*. | **Workaround:** none needed — the plan's own guardrail ("if the substrate diverges from the diagnosis, stop and surface") covers it, and the runtime baseline made the divergence provable in one `eval`. **Durable method:** when a diagnosis says "system X is dormant because its triggers are unauthored", verify the trigger's *enclosing collection is non-empty at runtime* before accepting content-starvation as the cause. One `eval state.<collection>.length` separates "nobody wrote the content" from "the reader is dead", and those have completely different remediations. Generalises the existing Debugging Protocol ("verify the noun before the verb") one level up: verify the *loop* before the *trigger*. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-724 implementation.

Every other Browser-pane tool (navigate, `read_page`, `javascript_tool`, `get_page_text`) worked fine, so the failure is specific to frame compositing. | ~6 min to rediscover and switch tools. | S | Yes — every UI-pillar ticket the unattended lane picks up. | **Workaround:** drive the DOM assertions through the Browser pane as usual, then take the screenshot itself with the Playwright MCP (`browser_resize` 1920×1080 → `browser_navigate` → `browser_take_screenshot`), which composites headlessly. **New 2026-07-23 (THR-727, hourly pickup) — tooling (M): `__DEBUG.fireAction()` immediately followed by `__DEBUG.tick()` silently loses the action — the React `setGameState` has not committed before the synchronous tick runs.** Verifying Divine Receipt, firing a player action then ticking to resolution produced no receipt: `fireAction` dispatches `setGameState(prev => {...add action})` (a React state update, applied asynchronously/batched), but `__DEBUG.tick(n)` drives `runTick` synchronously off the *current* committed state. In a tight `fireAction(); tick(12)` JS call the action is not yet in `state.unifiedActions` when the tick loop reads it, so it never progresses and never resolves — the fire is lost with zero error. ~40 fired actions across several attempts produced exactly one receipt (the one attempt that happened to `await d.clearTraces()` between fire and tick, which yielded to the event loop and let React flush). | ~25 min of "the phase must be broken" before isolating it to state-commit timing rather than the new phase. The phase was correct throughout. | M | Yes — any browser verification that fires an action via `__DEBUG` and advances via `__DEBUG.tick` in the same JS turn. | **Workaround:** `await new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,60))` between `fireAction()` and the first `tick()` so React commits the action into GameState; then tick in a loop with a short `await` per iteration if you also need to observe intermediate state. Also: player-fired actions have an RNG-rolled step duration (`mulberry32(seed + tick*43)`), so a 1-step action may still take several ticks — poll `listPlayerReceipts()` across ~15 single ticks rather than one big `tick(N)`. **Method note:** a receipt/aftermath that "never fires" in `__DEBUG` verification is a state-flush suspect before it is an engine suspect — confirm the action reached `state.unifiedActions` (or emitted an `agent_action_resolved` trace in a *fresh* buffer) before touching the producing phase. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-727 browser verification.

**New 2026-07-24 (THR-722, hourly pickup) — process (S): a retirement ticket's migration target can itself be dead, so "migrate X onto Y" needs a liveness check on Y before the interface-map row is written.** THR-722 specified migrating the dead edge `grants[]` onto `grantsTraitWhileHeld` or `effects[]`. `trait_grant` is the right substrate structurally (~20 catalog entries, a correct resolver collecting `grantedTraits`), but `hasGrantedTrait` has **zero production callers** and `resolveEffects().grantedTraits` is asserted only in tests — so the migration relocates the leak instead of closing it. Taking the ticket at face value would have produced a green-looking row for a contract still consuming nothing. **Workaround:** before writing a post-migration interface-map row, grep the *consumer* side of the target mechanism, not just its writers and resolver; badge LEAKED with a deferral (THR-737 here) when the read site is absent. The downgrade-only invariant in `scripts/interface-contracts.ts` is what makes this the cheap path — LIVE is never mechanical.

**New 2026-07-24 (THR-722, hourly pickup) — tooling (S): `doomIdentityMilestones.test.ts` "milestone triggered flag persists" times out at the default 5s under full-suite load but passes in ~2.1s in isolation.** It calls `initializeGameState` without the raised `MILESTONE_SMOKE_TIMEOUT_MS` its sibling test uses, so it is timing-sensitive rather than broken — the THR-489 red-baseline class. Cost a stash-and-rerun cycle to prove it pre-existing (identical `1 failed | 12981 passed` on a clean tree). **Workaround:** when a suite-only failure looks change-adjacent, `git stash push -u` and re-run the full suite before investigating; do not attribute it without that A/B. Real fix is giving the test the same explicit timeout as its sibling.

**Resolved 2026-07-24 (next hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, resuming THR-722).** The flake was not cosmetic: it is the *only* thing that failed CI on PR #779, so the armed auto-merge could never fire and THR-722 sat shipped-but-unmergeable. Fixed by passing `MILESTONE_SMOKE_TIMEOUT_MS` (already defined in the file) to the second `it`, pushed onto the PR branch. Full suite then went **866 files / 12982 tests, 0 failures** — the first fully-green local suite recorded in this log, which means the "1 known pre-existing failure" baseline everyone had been quoting *was* this flake and nothing else.

**Two lessons worth more than the one-line fix.** (1) **This is a recurrence of #142** — same file, same root cause, 6 weeks apart. THR-293's fix added `MILESTONE_SMOKE_TIMEOUT_MS` to the `it.each` block and left the plain `it` below it on the 5s default; that second test runs **40 ticks** (30 + 10) versus the parametrised one's 30, so the *slower* test was the one left unguarded. When adding a timeout to one test in a file, grep the whole file for sibling `it(`/`it.each(` calls doing comparable work. (2) **"Pre-existing, not mine" is a correct diagnosis but an incomplete action.** The prior run proved pre-existence rigorously and then shipped anyway with auto-merge armed — which cannot merge past a red required check, so the ticket silently stalled for an hour. If a pre-existing failure blocks *your* merge gate, fixing it is in scope; a clean A/B is the start of the job, not the end of it. | THR-722 resume, worktree `quirky-raman-5a4f75`.

**New 2026-07-24 (THR-718, hourly pickup) — tooling (S): proving an engine read-path wired in the browser needs a direct live-`WorldGraph` walk; the `__DEBUG` attachment helpers are too lossy for it.** Verifying that `stat_contribution` items feed `computeRawScore`, two `__DEBUG` helpers got in the way: (1) `spawnAttachment(agent, name)` matches by **partial name and takes the first hit** — `'Ashenmane'` spawned *Ashenmane Horse* (a mount I hadn't migrated) instead of *Ashenmane's Fang*, and `'Fang'` grabbed *Hollowfang*; only fully-unique names (`'Whispering Eye'`, `'Uncut Ruby'`, `'Burned Codex'`) spawned the intended item. Editing the two flagged wiki pages *in that other worktree* was rejected by `worktree-write-guard.sh` — the guard gates on the session CWD and treats any path outside it (including a sibling worktree) as "the home tree", and its suggested "corrected path" was a nonsensical nested path. Also: the two wiki pages are *generated* artifacts (`generate-design-wiki`) but body-preserving — hand-edits survive regeneration; `check:generated-freshness` "regenerating changed it" only means the edited page isn't committed yet (it diffs working-tree-after-prebuild vs committed HEAD), so the fix is simply to commit. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-718 PR-unstick.

Closeout: remove the junction with `cmd //c "rmdir node_modules"` (reparse point only) so the reaper's `git worktree remove --force` can never follow it into the home install. | Hourly `tb-opus-pickup` run, THR-721 worktree setup.

**New 2026-07-25 (THR-719, hourly pickup) — process (M): a plan's *predicted* payload-kind set was wrong in both directions, and only enumerating the real content caught it.** The THR-719 plan named four payload kinds to add (`condition_grant`, `self_remove`, `possession_grant`, `relationship_delta`) while explicitly instructing the executor to port “only the kinds authored content actually uses.” Enumerating the nine shipped `onUseTriggers` blocks showed `possession_grant` and `relationship_delta` had **zero** authored uses, and `condition_remove` — absent from the plan — had **two** (Amber Phial, Herb Bundle).
